Local-in-time well-posedness for the regular solution to the 2D full compressible Navier-Stokes equations with degenerate viscosities and heat conductivity
Yue Cao, Xun Jiang

Abstract
. This paper considers the two-dimensional Cauchy problem of the full compressible Navier-Stokes equations with far-field vacuum in ℝ 2 \mathbb{R}^{2} , where the viscosity and heat-conductivity coefficients depend on the absolute temperature θ \theta in the form of θ ν \theta^{\nu} with ν > 0 \nu>0 . Due to the appearance of the vacuum, the momentum equation are both degenerate in the time evolution and spatial dissipation, which makes the study on the well-posedness challenged. By establishing some new singular-weighted (negative powers of the density ρ \rho ) estimates of the solution, we establish the local-in-time well-posedness of the regular solution with far-field vacuum in terms of ρ \rho , the velocity u u and the entropy S S .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35Q30, 35A09, 35A01,
35B40, 76N10.

1.
Introduction

The full compressible Navier-Stokes equation (
FCNS
) describes the movement of viscous heat-conductive fluid, which is given by:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\rho_{t}+\text{div}(\rho u)=0,\\[5.0pt]
&(\rho u)_{t}+\text{div}(\rho u\otimes u)+\nabla P=\text{div}\mathbb{T},\\[5.0pt]
&(\rho\mathcal{E})_{t}+\text{div}(\rho\mathcal{E}u+Pu)=\text{div}(u\mathbb{T})+\text{div}(\kappa\nabla\theta).\end{aligned}\right.

(1.1)

In (
1.1
),
t\geq 0
and
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
are the time and the spatial coordinate, respectively,
\rho\geq 0
the density,
u=(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})^{\top}
the fluid velocity,
\theta
the absolute temperature,
\mathcal{E}=e+\frac{1}{2}|u|^{2}
the specific total energy,
e
the specific internal energy.
P
is the pressure, for polytropic fluids, the equation of state satisfies

P=R\rho\theta=(\gamma-1)\rho e=Ae^{S/c_{v}}\rho^{\gamma},\quad e=c_{v}\theta=\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\theta,

(1.2)

R>0
is the gas constant,
A
is a positive constant,
c_{v}>0
is the specific heat at unit volume,
\gamma>1
is the adiabatic exponent and
S
is the entropy.
\mathbb{T}
is the viscosity stress tensor given by

\mathbb{T}=2\mu D(u)+\lambda\text{div}u\mathbb{I}_{2},

(1.3)

where
D(u)=\frac{\nabla u+(\nabla u)^{\top}}{2}
is the deformation tensor,
\mathbb{I}_{2}
is the
2\times 2
identity matrix,
\mu
is the shear viscosity coefficient, and
\lambda+\mu
is the bulk viscosity coefficient.
\kappa
denotes the coefficient of heat conductivity.

For constant viscous heat-conductive flow, there is a lot of literature on the well-posedness and behavior of solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes equation.
When the initial data are away from vacuum, as demonstrated in
[
17
,
19
,
29
,
31
,
33
,
20
]
and so on, the local well-posedness of classical solutions to (
1.1
) can be established through the standard symmetric hyperbolic-parabolic structure that satisfies Kawashima’s condition.

When the initial vacuum is allowed, some new difficulties arise. For example, the degeneracy of the time evolution in both the momentum and energy equations made it difficult to characterize the behavior of
u
,
\theta
and
S
near the vacuum domain.
For the constant viscous flow with vacuum, Salvi and Stra
\rm{\check{s}}
kraba
[
30
]
first introduce the compatibility conditions and obtain the local-in-time existence of strong solutions to the isentropic flow, which has been extended to the non-isentropic flow by the work of Cho and Kim
[
6
]
. For the global-in-time existence of classical solutions, we refer to Huang, Li and Xin
[
16
]
for the isentropic flow in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
and Huang-Li
[
14
]
, Wen-Zhu
[
35
]
, for the non-isentropic flow in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Some other results can be refer to
[
28
,
13
,
26
,
18
,
27
]
and the references cited therein.

It was pointed that Xin-Zhu
[
37
]
and Duan-Xin-Zhu
[
8
]
reveal a phenomenon: the classical solutions with vacuum to the constant viscous heat-conductive flow cannot preserve the conservation of momentum. In fact, the assumption of constant viscosity leads to a nonphysical scenario, that is, the vacuum exerts an external force on the fluid at the vacuum boundary. Thus it is more appropriate to adopt degenerate viscosity coefficients to the viscous heat-conductive flows when modeling the vacuum problems.
Besides, according to the theory of gas dynamics, (
1.1
) can be derived from the Boltzmann equations through the Chapman-Enskog expansion
[
5
]
to the first order, and under some proper physical assumptions,
\mu
,
\lambda
and
\kappa
display as

\begin{split}\mu(\theta)=&\alpha\theta^{\mathrm{b}},\quad\lambda(\theta)=\beta\theta^{\mathrm{b}},\quad\kappa(\theta)=\hbar\theta^{\mathrm{b}},\end{split}

(1.4)

for

\mathrm{b}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{\Upsilon}\in\big[\frac{1}{2},\infty\big)
, where for Maxwellian molecules, the rigid elastic spherical molecules and the ionized gas,

\mathrm{b}=1,\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2}
, respectively.
For the isentropic flow, according to Boyle and Gay-Lussac law, the dependence on
\theta
is reduced to the dependence on
\rho
, i.e.

\mu(\rho)=\alpha\rho^{\mathrm{c}},\quad\lambda(\rho)=\beta\rho^{\mathrm{c}}.

(1.5)

For isentropic flow with density-dependent viscosities as in (
1.5
), the appearance of vacuum will cause the degenerate of both time evolution and spatial dissipation, and make it hard to establish the well-posedness theory.
Bresch and Desjardins
[
2
]
introduced the BD entropy identity,
which helps to establish the well-posedness of weak solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes equations, especially the study of weak solutions of the case with density-dependent viscosities, see
[
3
,
12
,
22
,
34
]
. When
\text{c}=1
in (
1.5
), Li-Pan-Zhu
[
24
]
established the local-in-time existence of classical solutions without using the BD entropy, see also Li-Pan-Zhu
[
25
]
for the case of
1<\text{c}\leq\frac{\gamma+1}{2}
; when
0<\text{c}<1
in (
1.5
), Xin-Zhu
[
37
]
obtain the local-in-time existence of classical solutions with conserved total mass and momentum in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. For the global existence of classical solutions, we refer to Cao-Li-Zhu
[
4
]
for the case in
\mathbb{R}
, Huang-Li
[
15
]
for the case in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and Xin-Zhu
[
36
]
for the case in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

For the degenerate non-isentropic flow with viscosity and heat-conductivity in (
1.4
), due to the appearance of the temperature in the viscous stress tensor and the heat flux vector, the difficulty of the problem will reach a new level. More precisely, for the isentropic flow, we can compare the density power to obtain the degeneracy of the time evolution and spatial dissipation, and then obtain the principal mathematical structure of the momentum equations. However, for the non-isentropic flow, the degeneracy of the time evolution is characterized by the power of the density, and the degeneracy of spatial dissipation is characterized by the power of the temperature, thus one needs to find a uniform quantity to evaluate the degeneracy. According to Boyle and Gay-Lussac law,

\theta=R^{-1}Ae^{S/c_{v}}\rho^{\gamma-1},

thus, one can rewrite the momentum equation with density and entropy, and the viscosity stress tensor term can be divided into two terms: one term with derivative on the viscosity coefficients, and one term with the derivative on the entropy. Then if we divide the momentum equation by
\rho
, the second term will become a more singular term than the isentropic case, and this makes the study on the non-isentropic case harder than the isentropic case.
Duan-Xin-Zhu
[
8
]

investigate the case with zero heat conductivity (
\kappa=0
), and establish the local-in-time existence of classical solutions in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where the solution has conserved total mass, momentum and energy, moreover,
S
maintains uniform upper and lower bounds throughout the spatial domain. Recently,
Duan-Xin-Zhu
[
9
]
extend the result to the case with positive heat conductivity (
\kappa>0
). However, due to the difference of interpolating estimates and singular-weighted estimates in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the theory in Duan-Xin-Zhu
[
9
]
for
\mathbb{R}^{3}
can not be directly apply to the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
cases.
This paper aims at address the local-in-time existence of classical solutions to the Cauchy problem of (
1.1
) with degenerate viscosity and far-field vacuum in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
where

\mu(\theta)=\alpha\theta^{\nu},\quad\lambda(\theta)=\beta\theta^{\nu},\quad\kappa=\hbar\theta^{\nu}

(1.6)

with constants
\alpha,\beta,\hbar,\nu
satisfying

\alpha>0,\quad\alpha+\beta\geq 0,\quad\hbar>0,\quad\text{and}\quad 0<\delta=(\gamma-1)\nu<1.

(1.7)

Then according to (
1.2
) and (
1.6
), one can rewrite (
1.1
) to the system of
(\rho,u,S)
as

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\rho_{t}+\text{div}(\rho u)=0,\\[1.0pt]
\displaystyle&(\rho u)_{t}+\text{div}(\rho u\otimes u)+\nabla P=A^{\nu}R^{-\nu}\text{div}\big(\rho^{\delta}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}\nu}Q(u)\big),\\[1.0pt]
\displaystyle&P\big(S_{t}+u\cdot\nabla S\big)-\digamma\rho^{\delta+\gamma-1}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}\nu}\Delta e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}\\
&\quad=A^{\nu}R^{1-\nu}\rho^{\delta}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}\nu}H(u)+\digamma\rho^{\delta}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}(\nu+1)}\Delta\rho^{\gamma-1}+\Lambda(\rho,S),\end{aligned}\right.

(1.8)

where
\digamma=R\hbar\big(\frac{A}{R}\big)^{\nu+1}
, and

\begin{split}Q(u)=&\alpha\big(\nabla u+(\nabla u)^{\top}\big)+\beta\text{div}u\mathbb{I}_{2},\quad H(u)=2\alpha|D(u)|^{2}+\beta(\text{div}u)^{2},\\
\Lambda(\rho,S)=&2\digamma\rho^{\delta}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}\nu}\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}\cdot\nabla e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}+\digamma\nabla(\rho^{\delta}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}\nu})\cdot\nabla(e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}\rho^{\gamma-1}).\end{split}

(1.9)

We will establish the local-in-time existence of
(\rho,u,S)
to the Cauchy problem
(
1.8
)
with (
1.2
), (
1.9
), and

\displaystyle(\rho,u,S)|_{t=0}=\big(\rho_{0}(x)>0,u_{0}(x),S_{0}(x)\big)\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(1.10)

\displaystyle(\rho,u,S)(t,x)\rightarrow(0,0,\bar{S})\quad\text{as}\quad|x|\rightarrow\infty\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad t\geq 0,

(1.11)

where
\bar{S}
is some constant.

In this paper, we adopt the following simplified notations for the standard homogeneous and inhomogeneous Sobolev spaces(see Galdi
[
11
]
):

\begin{split}&|f|_{p}=\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\quad\|f\|_{s}=\|f\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\quad\|f\|_{m,p}=\|f\|_{W^{m,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\[1.0pt]
&D^{k,r}=\{f\in L^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}):|f|_{D^{k,r}}=|\nabla^{k}f|_{r}<\infty\},\quad|f|_{D^{k,r}}=\|f\|_{D^{k,r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\[1.0pt]
&D^{k}=D^{k,2},\quad\int f=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f\text{d}x,\quad X([0,T];Y)=X\big([0,T];Y(\mathbb{R}^{2})\big),\\[1.0pt]
&\|f\|_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}=\|f\|_{X_{1}}+\|f\|_{X_{2}},\quad\|(f,g)\|_{X}=\|f\|_{X}+\|g\|_{X}.\end{split}

The
Lam
\acute{\text{e}}
operator
L
acting on
u
is defined as:

Lu\triangleq-\alpha\Delta u-(\alpha+\beta)\nabla\text{div}u.

Before stating the main results, we first introduce a proper class of solutions called regular solutions to the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
).

Definition 1.1
.

Let
T>0
be a finite constant.
(\rho,u,S)
is called a regular solution to the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
if

(1)

(\rho,u,S)
satisfies this problem in the sense of distributions;

(2)

\rho>0,\ \ \rho^{\gamma-1}\in C([0,T];L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}),\ \ \nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\in C([0,T];L^{q}\cap D^{1}),\\
\quad\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla^{3}\rho^{\delta-1}\in C([0,T];L^{2})
;

(3)

u\in C([0,T];H^{3})\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}),\quad\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla u\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\rho^{\frac{3(\delta-1)}{2}}\nabla^{2}u\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{1}),\quad\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{4}u\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
;

(4)

S-\bar{S}\in C([0,T];H^{3}),\quad e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}-e^{\frac{\bar{S}}{c_{v}}}\in C([0,T];H^{3})
,

for some constants
p,q\in(2,\infty)
.

Now, the main result in this paper can be stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Assume that

\gamma>1,\quad 0<\delta=(\gamma-1)\nu<1,\quad\gamma+\delta\leq 2,\quad\alpha>0,\quad\alpha+\beta\geq 0.

(1.12)

If
(\rho_{0},u_{0},S_{0})
satisfy

\displaystyle\rho_{0}>0,\quad\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}\in L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3},\quad\nabla\rho_{0}^{\delta-1}\in L^{q}\cap D^{1},\quad\rho_{0}^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla^{3}\rho_{0}^{\delta-1}\in L^{2},

(1.13)

\displaystyle\nabla\rho_{0}^{\frac{(\delta-1)}{2}}\in L^{4},\quad\nabla\rho_{0}^{\frac{2\gamma+\delta-3}{2}}\in L^{2},\quad u_{0}\in H^{3},\quad S_{0}-\bar{S}\in H^{3},

for some
p,q\in(2,\infty)
and the compatibility conditions:

\displaystyle u_{0}=\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\delta}{2}}g_{1},\ \nabla u_{0}=\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}g_{2},\ Lu_{0}=\rho_{0}^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}g_{3},\ \nabla(\rho^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}Lu_{0})=\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\delta}{2}}g_{4},

(1.14)

\displaystyle\nabla e^{\frac{S_{0}}{c_{v}}}_{0}=\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\delta}{2}}g_{5},\ \ \Delta e^{\frac{S_{0}}{c_{v}}}_{0}=\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}g_{6},\ \ \nabla(\rho^{\delta-1}_{0}\Delta e_{0}^{\frac{S_{0}}{c_{v}}})=\rho_{0}^{\frac{1-\delta}{2}}g_{7},

for
g_{i}\in L^{2}(i=1,\cdots,7)
, then the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) admits a unique strong solution
(\rho,u,S)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
for some time
T_{*}>0
with

\displaystyle\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{q}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}\right),\quad\rho_{t}^{\gamma-1}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{2}\right),

(1.15)

\displaystyle\rho_{tt}^{\gamma-1}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)\cap L^{2}(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}),\quad\nabla\rho_{t}^{\delta-1}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right),

\displaystyle\nabla\rho_{tt}^{\delta-1}\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),\quad u_{t}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla u_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),\quad(\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{2}u_{t},u_{tt})\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{t}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{3}\right),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{2}u_{t}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)

\displaystyle(t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}u_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla u_{tt})\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{tt}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}\right),

\displaystyle(S-\bar{S},\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla S,\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{2}S,\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}S)\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle S_{t}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right),\quad\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}S_{t}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}\right),

\displaystyle\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}S_{t}\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right),\quad S_{tt}\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)

\displaystyle(t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla^{2}S_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{1-\delta}{4}}S_{tt})\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),\;t^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{4}}\nabla S_{tt}\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right).

Moreover,
(\rho,u,S)
is a classical solution to the problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) and
\left(\rho,u,\theta=AR^{-1}\rho^{\gamma-1}e^{S/c_{v}}\right)
is a classical solution to the problem (
1.1
)-(
1.3
) with (
1.6
) and (
1.10
)-(
1.11
) in
\left(0,T_{*}\right]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Remark 1.1
.

(
1.13
)
-
(
1.14
)
identifies a class of admissible initial data that provide unique solvability to the problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
). Such initial data include

\rho_{0}(x)=\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{\varkappa}},\quad u_{0}(x)\in C^{3}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad S_{0}=\bar{S}+f(x),

for some
f(x)\in H^{3}
, where
\frac{3+\delta}{2}<\gamma+\delta\leq 2
,

\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{1}{p(\gamma-1)}<\varkappa<\frac{1-2/q}{2(1-\delta)}.

Remark 1.2
.

We make some comments on (
1.12
) for the physical parameters:

First,
\gamma+\delta\leq 2
is necessary to obtain the estimates of the density-related variables like
n=\rho^{2-\gamma-\delta}
. Moreover, the condition
\gamma+\delta\leq 2
allows more physical models compared to the condition
4\gamma+3\delta\leq 7
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Second,
0<\delta=\nu(\gamma-1)<1
includes many physical fluids, for example, taking
p=6
, then the example of the initial data includes the air (
\gamma=1.4,\ \nu=0.72
or
\nu=0.5
for high temperature), the carbon dioxide (
\gamma=\frac{4}{3},\ \nu=0.95
), the hydrogen (
\gamma=1.4,\ \nu=0.69
) etc. (See Chapter 2, Section 2.1, pages 128-129 of
[
23
]
and Chapter 19, pages 638-639 of
[
10
]
).

Remark 1.3
.

We remark that (
1.14
) are required in proving Theorem
1.1
, especially in deducing the singular-weighted estimates of the velocity and the entropy.
In particular,
in order to show that

\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}u,\ \rho^{\delta-1}\nabla u,\ \rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}u_{t},\ \rho^{\delta-1}\nabla u_{t},\ \rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}},\ (e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}})_{t},\ \rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla(e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}})_{t}

and then

\rho^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}\nabla^{2}u,\ \ \rho^{\frac{3(1-\delta)}{2}}\nabla^{3}u,\ \ \rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{2}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}},\ \ \rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}

are all belong to
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
for some constant
T>0
, we need the compatibility conditions (
1.14
).

In rest of this paper, we will introduce some basic facts in §2; and then the reformulation and main strategy for the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) will be given in §3; the linearization of the reformulated problem (
3.2
) is stated in §4; the uniform a priori estimates of the solutions to the linearized Cauchy problem (
4.1
) is established in §5; at last, we will prove the local-in-time existence of the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) in §6.

2.
Preliminaries

Now, we list some basic facts that will be frequently used in proving the local-in-time existence.
The first one is the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

[
21
]
Let
f\in L^{q_{1}}\cap D^{1,r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for
1\leq q_{1},r\leq\infty
. Suppose that real numbers
\xi
and
q_{2}
, and natural numbers
m
,
i
and
j
satisfy

\frac{1}{{q_{2}}}=\frac{j}{d}+\left(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{i}{2}\right)\xi+\frac{1-\xi}{q_{1}}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{j}{i}\leq\xi\leq 1.

Then
u\in D^{j,{q_{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and there exists a constant
C
depending only on
i
,
j
,
q_{1}
,
r
and
\xi
such that

\begin{split}\|\nabla^{j}f\|_{L^{{q_{2}}}}\leq C\|\nabla^{i}f\|_{L^{r}}^{\xi}\|f\|_{L^{q_{1}}}^{1-\xi}.\end{split}

(2.1)

Moreover, if
j=0
,
ir<2
and
q_{1}=\infty
, then it is necessary to make the additional assumption that either
f
tends to zero at infinity or that
f
lies in
L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some finite
s>0
;
if
1<r<\infty
and
i-j-2/r
is a non-negative integer, then it is necessary to assume also that
\xi\neq 1
.

More specifically, in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, it holds that

\begin{split}|f|_{3}\leq&C|\nabla f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{3}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad|f|_{4}\leq C|\nabla f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad|f|_{6}\leq C|\nabla f|_{2}^{\frac{2}{3}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{3}},\\
|f|_{\infty}\leq&C|\nabla f|_{3}^{\frac{3}{4}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq C|\nabla^{2}f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\nabla f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}},\quad|f|_{\infty}\leq C|\nabla f|_{3}^{\frac{2}{3}}|f|_{3}^{\frac{1}{3}},\\
|f|_{\infty}\leq&C|\nabla^{2}f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{3}}|f|_{4}^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad|f|_{2}\leq C|\nabla f|_{1}.\end{split}

The second one gives some compactness results.

Lemma 2.2
.

[
32
]
Let
X_{0}\subset X\subset X_{1}
be three Banach spaces. Suppose that
X_{0}
is compactly embedded in
X
and
X
is continuously embedded in
X_{1}
. Then it holds that

(1)

If
f
is bounded in
L^{r}([0,T];X_{0})
for
1\leq r<+\infty
, and
\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}
is bounded in
L^{1}([0,T];X_{1})
, then
f
is relatively compact in
L^{r}([0,T];X)
;

(2)

If
f
is bounded in
L^{\infty}([0,T];X_{0})
and
\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}
is bounded in
L^{r}([0,T];X_{1})
for
r>1
, then
f
is relatively compact in
C([0,T];X)
.

The third is used to obtain the time-weighted estimates of
u
.

Lemma 2.3
.

[
1
]

If
f(t,x)\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
, then there exists a sequence
s_{k}
such that

s_{k}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{and}\quad s_{k}|f(s_{k},x)|^{2}_{2}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as}\quad k\rightarrow\infty.

At last, one has the following regularity theory for
Lam
\acute{\text{e}}
problem:

-\alpha\Delta u-(\alpha+\beta)\nabla\text{div}u=f,\quad u\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad|x|\to\infty.

(2.2)

Lemma 2.4
.

[
7
]
If
u\in D^{1,r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
1<r<\infty
is a weak solution to (
2.2
), then

|u|_{D^{k+2,r}}\leq C|f|_{D^{k,r}},

where
C
depends only on
\alpha,\beta
and
r
.

3.
Reformulation and main strategy

3.1.
Reformulation

In order to prove Theorems
1.1
, we apply the density and entropy related functions

\displaystyle\phi=\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1},\quad l=e^{\frac{S}{c_{v}}}

(3.1)

to reformulate the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) as

\left\{\begin{aligned} \displaystyle&\phi_{t}+u\cdot\nabla\phi+(\gamma-1)\phi\text{div}u=0,\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle&u_{t}+u\cdot\nabla u+a_{1}\phi\nabla l+l\nabla\phi+a_{2}l^{\nu}\phi^{2\iota}Lu=a_{2}\phi^{2\iota}\nabla l^{\nu}\cdot Q(u)+a_{3}l^{\nu}\psi\cdot Q(u),\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle&l_{t}+u\cdot\nabla l-a_{4}\phi^{2\iota}l^{\nu}\Delta l=a_{5}l^{\nu}n\phi^{4\iota}H(u)+a_{6}l^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi+\Theta(\phi,l,\psi),\\
&(\phi,u,l)|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},u_{0},l_{0})=\Big(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\rho_{0}^{\gamma-1}(x),u_{0}(x),e^{\frac{S_{0}(x)}{c_{v}}}\Big)\quad\text{for}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
&(\phi,u,l)\rightarrow(0,0,\bar{l})\hskip 5.69054pt\text{as}\hskip 5.69054pt|x|\rightarrow\infty\quad\text{for}\quad t\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(3.2)

where
\psi=\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}
,
n=\rho^{2-\delta-\gamma}
,

\Theta(\phi,l,\psi)=a_{7}l^{\nu+1}\phi^{-2\iota}\psi\cdot\psi+a_{8}l^{\nu}\nabla l\cdot\psi+a_{9}l^{\nu-1}\phi^{2\iota}\nabla l\cdot\nabla l,

constants
\bar{l}=e^{\frac{\bar{S}}{c_{v}}}>0
,
\iota=\frac{\delta-1}{2(\gamma-1)}
, and

\displaystyle a_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma},\ \ a_{2}=a\Big(\frac{A}{R}\Big)^{\nu},\ \ a_{3}=\Big(\frac{A}{R}\Big)^{\nu},\ \ a_{4}=\digamma\frac{a}{Ac_{v}},\ \ a_{5}=\frac{A^{\nu-1}a^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{\nu}},

\displaystyle a_{6}=

\displaystyle\digamma\frac{(\gamma-1)}{Ac_{v}\delta},\ \ a_{7}=\digamma\frac{\gamma(\gamma-1)}{aAc_{v}\delta^{2}},\ \ a_{8}=2\digamma\frac{1+\nu}{Ac_{v}\nu},\ \ a_{9}=\digamma\frac{a\nu}{Ac_{v}},\ \ a=\Big(\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\Big)^{\frac{1-\delta}{\gamma-1}}.

We will first give the definition of regular solution and establish the local-in-time existence to the Cauchy problem (
3.2
).

Definition 3.1
.

(\phi,u,l)
is a strong solution to the Cauchy problem (
3.2
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, if it satisfies (
3.2
) in the sense of distributions and meets the equations in (
3.2
) a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Under
(
1.12
)
, for some
p,q\in(2,\infty)
and
\iota=\frac{\delta-1}{2(\gamma-1)}
, if
(\phi_{0},u_{0},l_{0})
satisfies the regularities:

\displaystyle\phi_{0}>0,\quad\phi_{0}\in L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3},\quad\nabla\phi^{2\iota}_{0}\in L^{q}\cap D^{1},\quad\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{3}\phi^{2\iota}_{0}\in L^{2},

(3.3)

\displaystyle\nabla\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}\iota}\in L^{4},\quad\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota+1}\in L^{2},\quad u_{0}\in H^{3},\quad l_{0}-\bar{l}\in H^{3},\quad\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}l_{0}>0,

and the compatibility conditions:

\displaystyle u_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{1},\quad\nabla u_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-2\iota}g_{2},\quad Lu_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-3\iota}g_{3},\quad\nabla(\phi_{0}^{3\iota}Lu_{0})=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{4},

(3.4)

\displaystyle\nabla l_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{5},\quad\Delta l_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-2\iota}g_{6},\quad\nabla(\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\Delta l_{0})=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{7},

for some
g_{i}(i=1,\cdots,7)\in L^{2}
, then there exist a time
T_{*}>0
and a unique strong solution
(\phi,u,l)
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
to the Cauchy problem (
3.2
) with

\displaystyle\phi(t,x)>0\quad\text{in}\;[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\displaystyle\inf_{(t,x)\in[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}l>0,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\phi\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}\right),\quad\nabla\phi^{\frac{1}{2}\iota}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\psi\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{q}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}\right),\;\phi_{t}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{2}\right),\;\psi_{t}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right),

\displaystyle\phi_{tt}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}\right),\quad\psi_{tt}\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle u\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{3}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{4}\right),\quad l-\bar{l}\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{3}\right),

\displaystyle(u_{t},l_{t})\in C\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right),\quad\phi^{3\iota}\nabla^{2}u\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];H^{1}\right),

\displaystyle\left(\phi^{2\iota}\nabla u,t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{4}u,\phi^{2\iota}\nabla u_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{t}\right)\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left(\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{4}u,\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{3}u_{t},u_{tt},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\nabla u_{tt},l_{tt},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{\iota}{2}}\nabla l_{tt}\right)\in L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{tt}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}\right),

\displaystyle\left(l-\bar{l},\phi^{\iota}\nabla l,\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l,\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{3}l,l_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{t},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{-\frac{\iota}{2}}l_{tt}\right)\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];L^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\phi^{\iota}l_{t}\in L^{\infty}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{1}\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\left[0,T_{*}\right];D^{2}\right).

Then based on Theorem 3.1, we can prove the local well-posedness to the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with (
1.2
) and (
1.9
)-(
1.11
) immediately.

3.2.
Main strategy

First, via establishing carefully designed singular-weighted estimates,
[
9
]
proved the local well-posedness of regular solutions to the corresponding Cauchy problem in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. However, the difference in the Sobolev embedding inequalities between
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathbb{R}^{3}
makes it hard to apply the same renormalization and singular-weighted estimates in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
to establish the well-posedness theory in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
For example, one has

|\phi^{\iota}f|_{6}\leq|\nabla(\phi^{\iota}f)|_{2}\leq C(|\nabla\phi^{\iota}|_{\infty}|f|_{2}+|\phi^{\iota}\nabla f|_{2})\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbb{R}^{3}

for some function
f
, where
\phi^{\iota}
is a quantity of negative power of
\rho
,
i.e.
|f|_{2},|\nabla\phi^{\iota}|_{\infty}
and
|\phi^{\iota}\nabla f|_{2}
are suffice to provide the singular-weighted estimate of
|\phi^{\iota}f|_{6}
, which plays key role in establishing uniform the a prior estimates, for example, in establishing the singular-weighted estimates of
l
, one has

\begin{split}\frac{a_{4}}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2}^{2}=&\underbrace{a_{5}\int ng^{\frac{3}{2}}H(v)_{t}l_{tt}}_{\text{trouble term}}+\cdots\\
\leq&C|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{6}|g^{\frac{3}{4}}\nabla v_{t}|_{3}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2}+\cdots\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+a_{4}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{tt}|_{2}^{2}=&\underbrace{a_{5}\int ng^{\frac{3}{2}}H(v)_{tt}l_{tt}}_{\text{trouble term}}+\cdots\\
\leq&C|n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{6}|g^{\frac{1}{4}}v_{tt}|_{3}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{tt}|_{2}+\cdots\end{split}

However, one has

|\phi^{\frac{\iota}{2}}f|_{4}\leq C\big(|\nabla\phi^{\iota}|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}|f|_{2}+|\phi^{\iota}\nabla f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|f|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbb{R}^{2},

which implies that, with
|f|_{2}
,
\nabla\phi^{\iota}|_{\infty}
and
|\phi^{\iota}\nabla f|_{2}
at hand,

f
can bear more singular weight
\phi^{\iota}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, but less singular weight
\phi^{\frac{\iota}{2}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
and this caused essential difficulties in deducing closed singular-weighted estimates, especially the estimates of
l
and
u
, and this is the reason why we need to find a different singular-weighted estimates in two dimensional space. See §
5
for more details.

To compensate for the distance in absorbing the singular weight caused by the dimension difference, we find that under a different linearized system, if we let
\phi
carry some singular weight, i.e. establish the singular-weighted estimates of
\phi
, then
u
and
l
can carry more singular weight of
\rho
than the
\mathbb{R}^{3}
case, which helps to establish the well-posedness in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We remark that the estimates in
[
9
]
do not provide the singular-weighted estimate of
\phi
, since one first needs to establish the equivalence between
h(=\phi^{2\iota})
and its linearized counterpart
g
, while this equivalence needs in turn the singular-weighted estimates of
\phi
. Here, we introduce the new quantity
h^{-1}\psi
, which satisfies

(h^{-1}\psi)_{t}+v\cdot\nabla(h^{-1}\psi)+(\nabla v)^{\top}h^{-1}\psi+a\delta\nabla\text{div}v=0,

and first deduce the
L^{\infty}
-estimates of
h^{-1}\psi
, then obtain the singular-weighted estimate of
\phi
without using the equivalence of
h
and
g
, see Lemma
5.3
for details.

At last, in order to establish the
L^{2}
estimate of
l-\bar{l}
, i.e. the
L^{2}
estimate of
S
, which is essential for demonstrating that the regular solution is indeed a classical one, the main difficulty is to deal with the singular term
a_{5}l^{\nu}n\phi^{4\iota}H(u)
in
\eqref{2.3}_{3}
.
Here, we introduce a new quantity
\phi^{2\iota}\nabla u
and establish its estimates in
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
by using the singular parabolic equations of
u
, which help to deal with difficulties due to the difference of embedding inequalities in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. See §
5.2.4
for more details.

4.
Linearization

This section states the linearized problem of (
3.2
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
for some constants
T>0
and
\epsilon>0
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\phi^{\epsilon}_{t}+v\cdot\nabla\phi^{\epsilon}+(\gamma-1)\phi^{\epsilon}\text{div}v=0,\\[2.0pt]
&u^{\epsilon}_{t}+v\cdot\nabla v+a_{1}\phi^{\epsilon}\nabla l^{\epsilon}+l^{\epsilon}\nabla\phi^{\epsilon}+a_{2}(l^{\epsilon})^{\nu}h^{\epsilon}Lu^{\epsilon}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad=a_{2}g\nabla(l^{\epsilon})^{\nu}\cdot Q(v)+a_{3}(l^{\epsilon})^{\nu}\psi^{\epsilon}\cdot Q(v),\\[2.0pt]
&l^{\epsilon}_{t}+v\cdot\nabla l^{\epsilon}-a_{4}w^{\nu}h^{\epsilon}\Delta l^{\epsilon}=a_{5}w^{\nu}n^{\epsilon}g^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi^{\epsilon}+\Pi(l^{\epsilon},h^{\epsilon},w,g),\\[2.0pt]
&(\phi^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},l^{\epsilon})|_{t=0}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0},u^{\epsilon}_{0},l^{\epsilon}_{0})=(\phi_{0}+\epsilon,u_{0},l_{0})\quad\text{for}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\\[2.0pt]
&(\phi^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},l^{\epsilon})\rightarrow(\epsilon,0,\bar{l})\quad\text{as}\hskip 5.69054pt|x|\rightarrow\infty\quad{\rm for}\quad t\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

where
h^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon})^{2\iota}
,
\psi^{\epsilon}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\epsilon}
,
n^{\epsilon}=(ah^{\epsilon})^{b}
,
b=\frac{2-\delta-\gamma}{\delta-1}\leq 0
, and

\Pi(l^{\epsilon},h^{\epsilon},w,g)=a_{7}w^{\nu+1}(h^{\epsilon})^{-1}\psi^{\epsilon}\cdot\psi^{\epsilon}+a_{8}w^{\nu}\nabla l^{\epsilon}\cdot\psi^{\epsilon}+a_{9}w^{\nu-1}g\nabla w\cdot\nabla w.

Here,
v=(v^{(1)},v^{(2)})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
g
and
w
are known functions satisfying
w>0
,
(v,g,w)(0,x)=(u_{0},h_{0}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{2\iota},l_{0})(x)
, and

\begin{split}&g\in L^{\infty}\cap C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),\ \ \nabla g\in C([0,T];L^{q}\cap D^{1}),\\[3.0pt]
&g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{3}g\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\ \ \nabla g^{\frac{1}{2}}\in C([0,T];L^{4}),\quad g_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{2}),\\[3.0pt]
&(\nabla g_{tt},v_{tt},w_{tt})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad v\in C([0,T];H^{3})\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}),\\[3.0pt]
&t^{\frac{1}{2}}v\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4}),\quad v_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}),\\[3.0pt]
&t^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3}),\quad w-\bar{l}\in C([0,T];H^{3}),\\[3.0pt]
&t^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{tt},w_{tt})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{1}),\quad\inf_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}w>0,\\[3.0pt]
&w_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2}).\end{split}

(4.2)

Moreover, we claim that
(\phi_{0},u_{0},l_{0})
and
\bar{l}=e^{\frac{\bar{S}}{c_{v}}}>0
in (
4.1
) is same as the ones in
(
3.2
)
.
Then according to the conventional theory
[
19
,
21
]
, one has the global well-posedness to (
4.1
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
for all
\epsilon>0
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under
(
1.12
)
, if
\epsilon>0
and
(\phi_{0},u_{0},l_{0})
satisfy
(
3.3
)-(
3.4
). Then
(
4.1
)
has a unique classical solution
(\phi^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},l^{\epsilon})
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
for all
T>0
with

\displaystyle\phi^{\epsilon}-\epsilon\in C([0,T];L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}),\quad\phi^{\epsilon}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{2}),

(4.3)

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}\cap C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad\nabla h^{\epsilon}\in C([0,T];H^{2}),

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{2}),\quad u^{\epsilon}\in C([0,T];H^{3})\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}),

\displaystyle u^{\epsilon}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}),\quad u^{\epsilon}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4}),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\epsilon}_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3}),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\epsilon}_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{1}),\quad l^{\epsilon}-\bar{l}\in C([0,T];H^{3}),

\displaystyle l^{\epsilon}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}),\quad l^{\epsilon}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}l_{t}^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2}),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}^{\epsilon}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{1}).

5.
Uniform a priori estimates

This section aims at establishing the uniform a priori estimates of the solution
(\phi^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},l^{\epsilon})
, which is independent of
\epsilon
.

First, for any fixed constant
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, as

(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0},u^{\epsilon}_{0},l^{\epsilon}_{0},h^{\epsilon}_{0})=\big(\phi_{0}+\epsilon,u_{0},l_{0},(\phi_{0}+\epsilon)^{2\iota}\big)
, and
(\phi_{0},u_{0},l_{0})
satisfy
(
3.3
)-(
3.4
), one has

\displaystyle 2+\epsilon+\bar{l}+\|\phi^{\epsilon}_{0}-\epsilon\|_{L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}}+\|\nabla h^{\epsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{q}\cap D^{1}}+|\nabla(\phi_{0}^{\epsilon})^{\iota+1}|_{2}

(5.1)

\displaystyle+|(h^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{3}h^{\epsilon}_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla(h^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{4}+|(h^{\epsilon}_{0})^{-1}|_{\infty}+\|u^{\epsilon}_{0}\|_{3}+|(l^{\epsilon}_{0})^{-1}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\|l^{\epsilon}_{0}-\bar{l}\|_{3}+\sum_{i=1}^{7}|g^{\epsilon}_{i}|_{2}\leq c_{0},

for some constant
c_{0}>1
independent of
\epsilon
, where

\begin{split}g^{\epsilon}_{1}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\iota}u^{\epsilon}_{0},\quad g^{\epsilon}_{2}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{2\iota}\nabla u^{\epsilon}_{0},\quad g_{3}^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{3\iota}Lu^{\epsilon}_{0},\quad g_{4}^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla\big((\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{3\iota}Lu^{\epsilon}_{0}\big),\\
g_{5}^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}^{\epsilon},\quad g_{6}^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{2\iota}\Delta l^{\epsilon}_{0},\quad g_{7}^{\epsilon}=(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla\big((\phi^{\epsilon}_{0})^{2\iota}\Delta l^{\epsilon}_{0}\big).\end{split}

Hereinafter, denote
M(c)\geq 1(c\geq 0)
a generic increasing and continuous function,
C\geq 1
a generic constant, which are differ from one line to another and only depend on constants
A,R,c_{v},\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,T
.
Moreover,
for simplicity in statement, we still denote

(\phi^{\epsilon}_{0},u^{\epsilon}_{0},l^{\epsilon}_{0},h^{\epsilon}_{0},\psi^{\epsilon}_{0})
,

(\phi^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},l^{\epsilon},h^{\epsilon},\psi^{\epsilon})
, and
g_{i}^{\epsilon}(i=1,\cdots,7)
as the ones without the superscript
\epsilon
, and
(\phi,u,l)
is the unique solution to
(
4.1
)
.

Now, for
g
,
w
and
v
, let constants
c_{i}(i=1,\cdots,5)
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(\|\nabla g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{q}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}}+|\nabla g^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{4}^{2})(t)\leq c_{1}^{2},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\inf_{[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}w(t,x)\geq c^{-1}_{1},\quad\inf_{[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}g(t,x)\geq c_{1}^{-1},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|w|^{2}_{\infty}+|v|_{\infty}^{2})(t)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}(|v|^{2}_{D^{2}}+|v_{t}|^{2}_{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{1}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(\|v\|^{2}_{1}+|g\nabla v|_{2}^{2}+|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{2}^{2})(t)+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}(|w_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|g\nabla^{2}w|_{2}^{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|w_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|g\nabla^{2}w|_{2}^{2})(t)+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}(|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|g\nabla^{3}w|_{2}^{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|g\nabla^{3}w|_{2}^{2})(t)+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}(|w_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\text{ess}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}t(|g^{-\frac{1}{4}}w_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{2})(t)+\int_{0}^{T^{*}}t|g^{\frac{1}{4}}w_{tt}|_{D^{1}}^{2}\text{d}t\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|v|^{2}_{D^{2}}+|v_{t}|^{2}_{2}+|g^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla^{2}v|^{2}_{2})(t)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}(|v|_{D^{3}}^{2}+|v_{t}|^{2}_{D^{1}})\text{d}t\leq c_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|v|^{2}_{D^{3}}+|v_{t}|_{D^{1}}^{2}+|g\nabla v_{t}|^{2}_{2}+|g_{t}|^{2}_{D^{1}})(t)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}(|v|^{2}_{D^{4}}+|v_{t}|^{2}_{D^{2}}+|v_{tt}|^{2}_{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}(|g^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla^{3}v|^{2}_{2}+|g_{t}|^{2}_{\infty})(t)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}(|(g\nabla^{2}v)_{t}|^{2}_{2}+|g\nabla^{4}v|^{2}_{2})\text{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\text{ess}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}t(|v|^{2}_{D^{4}}+|g\nabla^{2}v_{t}|^{2}_{2})(t)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}(|g_{tt}|_{4}^{2}+|g_{tt}|^{2}_{D^{1}})\text{d}t\leq c^{2}_{5},

\displaystyle\text{ess}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}t|v_{tt}(t)|^{2}_{2}+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}t(|v_{tt}|^{2}_{D^{1}}+|g^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{tt}|_{D^{1}}^{2}+|v_{t}|^{2}_{D^{3}})\text{d}t\leq c_{5}^{2},

for some time
T^{*}\in(0,T]
, where

1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}\leq c_{3}\leq c_{4}\leq c_{5},

(5.3)

and
T^{*}
,
c_{i}(i=1,\cdots,5)
will be determined later, which depend only on
c_{0}
and fixed constants
A,R,c_{v},\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,T
.

5.1.
Initial information

First, we state some initial regularity based on (
5.1
) and compatibility conditions, which will be used in the a priori estimates of
\phi
and
u
.

Lemma 5.1
.

It follows from (
5.1
) and compatibility conditions that

|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{3}u_{0}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{3}l_{0}|_{2}\leq\hat{C},

where
\hat{C}>0
is a generic constant that depends on
c_{0}
but independent of
\epsilon
.

Proof.

First, according to the definition of
g_{3}
,
\phi_{0}>\epsilon
and
\eqref{ln}_{5}
that

\displaystyle L(\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0})=g_{3}-\frac{\delta-1}{a\delta}G(\phi_{0},\psi_{0},u_{0}),\quad\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}\longrightarrow 0\ \ \text{as}\ \ |x|\longrightarrow\infty,

where

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}G(\phi_{0},\psi_{0},u_{0})=

\displaystyle(\alpha+\beta)\big(\phi_{0}^{\iota}\psi_{0}{\rm{div}}u_{0}+\phi_{0}^{\iota}\psi_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{0}+u_{0}\cdot\nabla(\phi_{0}^{\iota}\psi_{0})\big)

(5.4)

\displaystyle+\alpha\phi_{0}^{\iota}\psi_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{0}+\alpha{\rm{div}}\big(u_{0}\otimes\phi_{0}^{\iota}\psi_{0}\big).

Then according to Lemma
2.4
and
(
5.1
)
that

\begin{split}|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}|_{D^{2}}\leq&C(|g_{3}|_{2}+|G(\phi_{0},\psi_{0},u_{0})|_{2})\leq C_{1}<\infty,\\
|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{2}\leq&C\big(|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}|_{D^{2}}+|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}u_{0}|_{4}\\
&+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}(|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}|u_{0}|_{4})\big)\leq C_{1}<\infty,\end{split}

(5.5)

for some generic constant
C_{1}>0
that independent of
\epsilon
.
Moreover, one has

\begin{split}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}|_{2}\leq C_{1}.\end{split}

Second, since

\nabla(\phi_{0}^{3\iota}Lu_{0})=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{4}\in L^{2}
,
thus formally,

\displaystyle L(\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0})=\Delta^{-1}{\rm{div}}\big(\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{4}\big)-\frac{\delta-1}{a\delta}G(\phi_{0},\psi_{0},u_{0}),

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}\longrightarrow 0\ \ \text{as}\ \ |x|\longrightarrow\infty,

this implies

\begin{split}|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}|_{D^{3}}\leq&C(|\phi_{0}^{-\iota}|_{\infty}|g_{4}|_{2}+|G(\phi_{0},\psi_{0},u_{0})|_{D^{1}})\leq C_{1}<\infty,\\
|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{3}u_{0}|_{2}\leq&C\big(|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}u_{0}|_{D^{3}}+|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}\\
&+|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}(|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla u_{0}|_{4}+|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|u_{0}|_{\infty})\\
&+|\psi_{0}|^{2}_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{-3\iota}|_{\infty}|u_{0}|_{2}+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{2}\\
&+|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}|_{2}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}u_{0}|_{\infty}\big)\leq C_{1}.\end{split}

(5.6)

Similarly, the definition of
g_{6}
and
\phi_{0}>\epsilon
imply that

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta\big(\phi_{0}^{2\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})\big)=g_{6}+2\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\cdot\nabla l_{0}+(l_{0}-\bar{l})\Delta\phi_{0}^{2\iota},\\
\phi_{0}^{2\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }|x|\longrightarrow\infty,\end{array}

which, together with the standard elliptic theory and
(
5.1
)
, yields that

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})|_{D^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|g_{6}|_{2}+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla l_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}-\bar{l}|_{\infty}|\nabla^{2}h_{0}|_{2}\big)\leq C_{1}<\infty,

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})|_{D^{2}}+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla l_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left|l_{0}-\bar{l}\right|_{\infty}|\nabla^{2}h_{0}|_{2}\big)\leq C_{1}<\infty.

At last, since

\nabla(\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\Delta l_{0})=\phi_{0}^{-\iota}g_{7}\in L^{2}
,
thus formally

\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta\big(\phi_{0}^{3\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})\big)=\Delta^{-1}{\rm{div}}(g_{7}+\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}\cdot\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\Delta l_{0})+2\nabla\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\cdot\nabla l_{0}\\
+(l_{0}-\bar{l})\Delta\phi_{0}^{3\iota},\\
\phi_{0}^{3\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }\quad|x|\longrightarrow\infty,\end{array}

which implies

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})|_{D^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(|g_{7}|_{2}+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|(h_{0})^{-1}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}|g_{6}|_{2}+\aleph)\leq C_{1},

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{3}l_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}(l_{0}-\bar{l})|_{D^{3}}+\aleph)\leq C_{1},

where

\displaystyle\aleph=

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla l_{0}|_{4}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}+|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}|\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}^{2\iota}|_{4}

\displaystyle+|l_{0}-\bar{l}|_{\infty}(|(h_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla^{3}h_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}|\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}^{2\iota}|_{4}+|\phi_{0}^{-\iota}|_{\infty}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}^{2}).

The proof of Lemma
5.1
is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

It holds that

\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\big(|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}+|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}+|h^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{t}(\tau)|_{2}+|h\nabla u_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\big)\leq M(c_{0}).

Proof.

First,
(3.1)_{3}
gives

\displaystyle|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\big(|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}\big(-v\cdot\nabla l+a_{4}w^{\nu}h\Delta l

\displaystyle+a_{5}w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi+\Pi(l,h,w,g)\big)|_{2}\big)(\tau),

then Lemmas
4.1
-
5.1
and (
5.1
) imply that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|l_{0}^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}u_{0}\cdot\nabla l_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{2}}h_{0}\Delta l_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{2}}h_{0}^{b}h_{0}^{2}H(u_{0})|_{2}

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{1+\frac{\nu}{2}}\text{div}\psi_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}\Pi(l_{0},h_{0},w_{0},g_{0})|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|l_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}(|l_{0}^{-\nu}|_{\infty}|u_{0}|_{\infty}|\nabla l_{0}|_{2}+|g_{6}|_{2}

\displaystyle+|\phi_{0}^{2b\iota}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}^{2})+C|l_{0}^{1+\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}(|\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}^{2\iota}|_{2}+|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}^{2})

\displaystyle+C|l_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}+C|l_{0}^{\frac{\nu}{2}-1}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}^{2}\leq M(c_{0}).

Moreover,
(3.1)_{3}
gives

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mid h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\big(-v\cdot\nabla l+a_{4}w^{\nu}h\Delta l

\displaystyle+a_{5}w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi+\Pi\big)|_{2}(\tau),

thus (
4.2
), (
5.1
) and Lemmas
4.1
-
5.1
imply that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{t}(\tau)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\left(u_{0}\cdot\nabla l_{0}\right)|_{2}+|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l_{0}^{\nu}h_{0}\Delta l_{0})|_{2}

\displaystyle+\mid h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\big(l_{0}^{\nu}h_{0}^{b}h_{0}^{2}H(u_{0})\big)|_{2}+|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l_{0}^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi_{0})|_{2}+|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\Pi_{0}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|u_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla u_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{3}l_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle+|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}^{\nu}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{4}+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{-\iota}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle+|\phi_{0}^{2b\iota}|_{\infty}(|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}^{2}+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{3\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{4})

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}(|\phi_{0}^{(2b-1)\iota}|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}^{2}+|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4})

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu+1}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{-2\iota}|_{\infty}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}^{2}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu+1}|_{\infty}(|\phi_{0}^{-\iota}|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}^{2}|\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}|_{\infty}+|\nabla\phi_{0}^{\iota}|_{4}|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4})

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{2}|\nabla l_{0}|_{\infty}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}+|l_{0}^{\nu-2}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}(|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}+|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4})

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}(|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{2}|\nabla l_{0}|_{\infty}+|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{4})\big)\leq M(c_{0}).

Second,
(3.1)_{2}
gives

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{K}(\tau)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(|v|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v|_{2}+|\phi|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}+|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\phi|_{2}|l|_{\infty}+|l^{\nu}|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{3}{2}}Lu|_{2}

\displaystyle+|l^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}+|\psi|_{\infty}|l^{\nu}|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v|_{2})(\tau),

where
\mathcal{K}=v\cdot\nabla v+a_{1}\phi\nabla l+l\nabla\phi+a_{2}hl^{\nu}Lu-a_{2}\nabla l^{\nu}\cdot gQ(v)-a_{3}l^{\nu}\psi\cdot Q(v)
, thus (
4.2
), (
5.1
), (
5.5
), (
5.6
) and Lemma
4.1
imply that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(|u_{0}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla u_{0}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla\phi_{0}\right|_{2}\left|l_{0}\right|_{\infty}+\left|l_{0}^{\nu}\right|_{\infty}\left|g_{3}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\psi_{0}\right|_{\infty}|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla u_{0}\right|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}\right|_{2})\leq M(c_{0}).

At last, according to
h_{0}l_{0}^{\nu}(h\nabla Lu_{0}+Lu_{0}\otimes\nabla h_{0})=l_{0}^{\nu}g_{4}
,

\eqref{ln}_{2}
(
4.2
), (
5.1
), and Lemmas
4.1
-
5.1
that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}|h\nabla u_{t}(\tau)|_{2}\leq\limsup_{\tau\rightarrow 0}|h\nabla\mathcal{K}(\tau)|_{2}

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|u_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{4}+|\nabla u_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{2}+|l_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle+|\phi_{0}^{2\iota+1}\nabla^{2}l_{0}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla\phi_{0}|_{4}+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}(|\nabla\psi_{0}|_{4}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}

\displaystyle+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{2})+|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\nabla u_{0}|_{4}|\nabla l_{0}|_{4}

\displaystyle+|l_{0}^{\nu}|_{\infty}|g_{4}|_{2}+|l_{0}^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|h_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}Lu_{0}|_{4}|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{0}|_{4}+|h_{0}\nabla^{2}l_{0}^{\nu}|_{2}|h_{0}\nabla u_{0}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{0}^{\nu}|_{4}(|h_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla^{2}u_{0}|_{4}+|\psi_{0}|_{\infty}|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u_{0}|_{4})\big)\leq M(c_{0}).

The proof of Lemma
5.2
is complete.
∎

5.2.
Uniform a priori estimates

5.2.1.
The estimates on the density-related variables

Let

\varphi=h^{-1},\quad\zeta=\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad n=(ah)^{b}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h.

Lemma 5.3
.

Set
T_{1}=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4})^{-1}\}
. For
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
, one has

\displaystyle h>\frac{1}{2c_{0}},\quad\frac{2}{3}\epsilon^{-2\iota}<\varphi<2c_{0},\quad\widetilde{C}^{-1}\leq gh^{-1}\leq\widetilde{C},

(5.9)

where
\widetilde{C}
is a suitable constant independent of
\epsilon
and
c_{i}(i=0,\cdots,5)
.

Proof.

First,
estimates on
\varphi=h^{-1}
.

Note that

\varphi_{t}+v\cdot\nabla\varphi+(1-\delta)\varphi\text{div}v=0,

then along the particle path
\mathbb{X}(t;x)

\displaystyle\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}s}\mathbb{X}(t;x)=v\big(s,\mathbb{X}(t;x)\big),\quad 0\leq t\leq T;\quad\mathbb{X}(0;x)=x,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(5.10)

one has

\begin{split}\displaystyle\varphi\big(t,\mathbb{X}(t;x)\big)=\varphi_{0}(x)\exp\Big((\delta-1)\int_{0}^{t}\text{div}v\big(s,\mathbb{X}(s;x)\big)\text{d}s\Big).\end{split}

This, along with (
5.2
), implies that

\begin{split}\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\epsilon^{-2\iota}<\varphi(t,x)<2|\varphi_{0}|_{\infty}\leq 2c_{0}\quad\text{for}\quad(t,x)\in[0,T_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\end{split}

(5.11)

and

h>\frac{1}{2c_{0}}\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

Before moving to prove the equivalence of
g
and
h
in short time, we first give the estimate of
h^{-1}\nabla h
, i.e.
h^{-1}\psi
.
Notice that

(h^{-1}\psi)_{t}+v\cdot\nabla(h^{-1}\psi)+(\nabla v)^{\top}h^{-1}\psi+a\delta\nabla\text{div}v=0,

along with the particle path (
5.10
), one has

\begin{split}(h^{-1}\psi)\big(t,&\mathbb{X}(t;x)\big)=\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla v)^{\top}\big(s,\mathbb{X}(s;x)\big)\text{d}s\right)\\
&\cdot\left(\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}h_{0}^{-1}\nabla h_{0}-a\delta\int_{0}^{t}\nabla\text{div}v\exp\int_{0}^{s}(\nabla v)^{\top}\text{d}\tau\big(s,\mathbb{X}(s;x)\big)\text{d}s\right),\end{split}

(5.12)

this together with (
5.2
) implies that

|h^{-1}\psi|_{\infty}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

(5.13)

Second, denoting
gh^{-1}=y(t,x)
, then

y_{t}+yh_{t}h^{-1}=g_{t}h^{-1},\quad y(0,x)=1,

thus

y(t,x)=\exp\left(-\int^{t}_{0}h_{s}h^{-1}\text{d}s\right)\left(1+\int^{t}_{0}g_{s}h^{-1}\exp\big(\int^{s}_{0}h_{\tau}h^{-1}\text{d}\tau\big)\text{d}s\right),

(5.14)

where

h_{t}h^{-1}=-v\cdot\nabla hh^{-1}-(\delta-1)\text{div}v,

and by (
5.2
), (
5.13
), it holds that

\begin{split}|h_{t}h^{-1}|_{\infty}\leq&C\big(|v|_{\infty}|h^{-1}\nabla h|_{\infty}+|\nabla v|_{\infty}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4},\end{split}

(5.15)

this, together with (
5.2
), (
5.11
) and (
5.14
),
implies that

\widetilde{C}^{-1}\leq hg^{-1}(t)\leq\widetilde{C}

(5.16)

for
0\leq t\leq T_{1}=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4})^{-1}\}
.
Moreover,

|g^{-1}|_{\infty}=|hg^{-1}\varphi|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}|\varphi|_{\infty}\leq Cc_{0}.

Based on (
5.16
), we will not distinguish
h
and
g
in the rest of the a priori estimates.
The proof of Lemma
5.3
is complete.

∎

5.2.2.
Estimates on
\phi
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Set
T_{2}=\min\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{4})^{-2}\}
. Then for
t\in[0,T_{2}]
, one has

\displaystyle|\phi|_{\infty}+|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\phi|_{2}+\|\phi-\epsilon\|_{L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}\leq Cc_{0},\ \ |h\nabla^{2}\phi|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{2},\ \ |h^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{t}|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{2},

\displaystyle|h\nabla\phi_{t}|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{3},\quad|\phi_{t}|_{D^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{4},\quad|\phi_{tt}|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}c_{4}^{2},\quad\int^{t}_{0}\|\phi_{ss}\|^{2}_{1}{\rm{d}}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4}^{2}.

Proof.

First, it follows from
\eqref{ln}_{1}

and
(
5.2
)
, one has

\displaystyle\|\phi-\epsilon\|_{L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|\phi_{0}-\epsilon\|_{L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\nabla v\|_{3}\text{d}s\Big)

(5.17)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\Big(\int^{t}_{0}C\|v\|_{3}\text{d}s\Big)\leq Cc_{0},

for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
. Moreover,

|\phi|_{\infty}=|\phi-\epsilon+\epsilon|_{\infty}\leq|\phi-\epsilon|_{\infty}+\epsilon\leq C\|\phi-\epsilon\|_{L^{p}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}+\epsilon\leq Cc_{0}.

(5.18)

Similarly, through the standard energy estimates, and

\begin{split}h^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}+v\cdot\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\delta-1}{2}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\text{div}v=0,\qquad h_{t}+v\cdot\nabla h+(\delta-1)h\text{div}v=0,\end{split}

it is easy to deduce from
\eqref{ln}_{1}
,
(
5.2
)
and Lemma
5.1
-
5.3
that

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\phi|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(|\phi^{\iota}_{0}\nabla\phi_{0}|_{2}+\int^{t}_{0}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}v|_{2}\text{d}s\Big)\exp{\left(\int_{0}^{t}C\|v\|_{3}\text{d}s\right)}\leq Cc_{0},

(5.19)

\displaystyle|h\nabla^{2}\phi|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(|\phi^{2\iota}_{0}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}|_{2}+\int^{t}_{0}(|h\nabla^{2}v|_{2}+|h\nabla^{3}v|_{2})\text{d}s\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp{\left(\int_{0}^{t}C\|v\|_{3}\text{d}s\right)}\leq Cc_{0}^{2},

for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
.

Then, according to
\eqref{ln}_{1}
, (
5.2
) and (
5.17
)-(
5.19
), for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
, one has

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|_{2}\leq C(|v|_{4}|\nabla\phi|_{4}+|\phi|_{\infty}|\nabla v|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}c_{2},

(5.20)

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|_{D^{1}}\leq C(|v|_{\infty}|\nabla^{2}\phi|_{2}+|\nabla\phi|_{4}|\nabla v|_{4}+|\phi|_{\infty}|\nabla^{2}v|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}c_{3},

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{t}|_{2}\leq C(|v|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\phi|_{2}+|\phi|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{2},

\displaystyle|h\nabla\phi_{t}|_{2}\leq C(|v|_{\infty}|h\nabla^{2}\phi|_{2}+|\nabla\phi|_{4}|h\nabla v|_{4}+|\phi|_{\infty}|h\nabla^{2}v|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{3},

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|_{D^{2}}\leq C\|v\|_{3}(\|\nabla\phi\|_{2}+|\phi|_{\infty})\leq Cc_{0}c_{4}.

Second, applying
\partial_{t}
to
\eqref{ln}_{1}
,
using (
5.2
), (
5.17
)-(
5.20
), for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
, one has

\displaystyle|\phi_{tt}|_{2}\leq C(|v_{t}|_{4}|\nabla\phi|_{4}+|v|_{\infty}|\nabla\phi_{t}|_{2}+|\phi_{t}|_{4}|\nabla v|_{4}+|\phi|_{\infty}|\nabla v_{t}|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle\|\phi_{ss}\|^{2}_{1}\text{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|(v\cdot\nabla\phi)_{s}\|_{1}^{2}+\|(\phi{\mathrm{div}}v)_{s}\|_{1}^{2}\big)\text{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4}^{2}.

The proof of Lemma
5.4
is complete.

∎

5.2.3.
Estimates on
\psi
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Set
T_{3}=\min\{T_{2},(1+Cc_{4})^{-5}\}
. Then for
t\in[0,T_{3}]
and
2<q<\infty
, one has

\begin{split}|\psi|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{q}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{2},\quad|\psi_{t}|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}c_{3},\quad|\psi_{t}|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\int^{t}_{0}|\psi_{ss}|^{2}_{2}{\rm{d}}s\leq Cc_{4}^{4},\\
|h_{t}|_{\infty}\leq M(c_{0})c_{3}^{\frac{3}{4}}c_{4}^{\frac{1}{4}},\quad\int_{0}^{t}|h_{s}|_{\infty}^{2}{\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0}),\quad\int_{0}^{t}|h_{ss}|_{4}^{2}{\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0})c_{4}^{2}.\end{split}

Proof.

First, it follows from
\eqref{ln}_{1}
that

\psi_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{2}A_{k}(v)\partial_{k}\psi+B(v)\psi+a\delta h\nabla\text{div}v=0,

(5.21)

where
A_{k}(v)=(a^{k}_{ij})_{2\times 2}
for
i
,
j
,
k=1,2
,
are symmetric with

a^{k}_{ij}=v^{(k)}\quad\text{for}\ i=j;\ \text{otherwise}\ a^{k}_{ij}=0,
and
B(v)=(\nabla v)^{\top}+(\delta-1)\text{div}v\mathbb{I}_{2}
.
Then, the classical energy estimate on (
5.21
) and Lemma
5.3
imply

\begin{split}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|\psi|^{q}_{q}\leq&C(|\nabla v|_{\infty}|\psi|^{q}_{q}+|hg^{-1}|_{\infty}|g\nabla^{2}v|_{q}|\psi|_{q}^{q-1})\\
\leq&C(|\nabla v|_{\infty}|\psi|^{q}_{q}+\|g\nabla^{2}v\|_{1}|\psi|_{q}^{q-1}),\end{split}

(5.22)

this, together with (
5.1
), (
5.2
), (
5.22
) and Gronwall’s inequality, gives

|\psi|_{q}\leq Cc_{0}\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{3}.

(5.23)

Similarly, one can deduce that

\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}\|\psi\|_{D^{1}\cap D^{2}}\leq C\big(c_{4}\|\psi\|_{D^{1}\cap D^{2}}+|g\nabla^{4}v|_{2}+c_{4}\big),

this, together with (
5.1
) and Gronwall’s inequality give that for
0\leq t\leq T_{3}
,

\begin{split}\|\psi\|_{D^{1}\cap D^{2}}\leq&\Big(c_{0}+Cc_{4}t+C\int_{0}^{t}|g\nabla^{4}v|_{2}\text{d}s\Big)\exp(Cc_{4}t)\leq Cc_{0}.\end{split}

(5.24)

Moreover, (
5.23
)-(
5.24
) and Lemma
2.1
imply

|\psi|_{\infty}\leq Cc_{0},\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{3}.

(5.25)

Third, according to (
5.2
), (
5.15
),
(
5.21
)
, (
5.23
)-(
5.25
), for
0\leq t\leq T_{3}
, one has

\displaystyle|\psi_{t}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|v|_{\infty}|\nabla\psi|_{2}+|\nabla v|_{2}|\psi|_{\infty}+|hg^{-1}|_{\infty}|g\nabla^{2}v|_{2}\big)\leq Cc_{0}c_{3},

\displaystyle|\psi_{t}|_{D^{1}}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|v\|_{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{q}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{2}}+|hg^{-1}|_{\infty}|g\nabla^{3}v|_{2}\big)\leq Cc^{2}_{4},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\psi_{ss}|^{2}_{2}\text{d}s\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\big(|v_{s}|^{2}_{4}|\nabla\psi|^{2}_{4}+|\nabla v|^{2}_{\infty}|\psi_{s}|^{2}_{2}+|v|^{2}_{\infty}|\nabla\psi_{s}|^{2}_{2}+|\psi|^{2}_{\infty}|\nabla v_{s}|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle+|hg^{-1}|_{\infty}^{2}(|h^{-1}h_{t}|_{\infty}^{2}|g\nabla^{2}v|_{2}^{2}+|g\nabla^{2}v_{s}|^{2}_{2})\big)\text{d}s\leq Cc^{4}_{4}.

At last, it follows from (
5.2
), the estimates on
\psi
, Lemma
5.3
and

\begin{split}h_{t}=-v\cdot\nabla h-(\delta-1)h\text{div}v,\quad h_{tt}=&-(v\cdot\nabla h)_{t}-(\delta-1)(h\text{div}v)_{t},\end{split}

that for
0\leq t\leq T_{3}

\begin{split}|h_{t}|_{\infty}\leq&C(|v|_{\infty}|\psi|_{\infty}+|h\nabla v|_{\infty})\leq M(c_{0})c_{3}^{\frac{3}{4}}c_{4}^{\frac{1}{4}},\\
\int_{0}^{t}|h_{ss}|_{4}^{2}\text{d}s\leq&C\int_{0}^{t}\big(|\psi|_{\infty}^{2}|v_{s}|_{4}^{2}+|\psi_{s}|_{4}^{2}|v|_{\infty}^{2}+|h_{s}|^{2}_{\infty}|\nabla v|_{2}^{2}+|h\nabla v_{s}|_{4}^{2}\big)\text{d}s\\
\leq&M(c_{0})c_{4}^{2}.\end{split}

The proof of Lemma
5.5
is complete.

∎

Lemma 5.6
.

For
t\in[0,T_{3}]
and
q>2
, one has

\displaystyle|\zeta|_{4}+|n|_{\infty}+|\nabla n|_{\infty}+\|n\|_{D^{1,q}\cap D^{1,4}\cap D^{2}\cap D^{3}}\leq M(c_{0}),\quad|n_{t}|_{2}\leq M(c_{0})c_{2},

\displaystyle|n_{t}|_{\infty}+|\nabla n_{t}|_{2}+|\nabla n_{t}|_{4}\leq M(c_{0})c_{4},\quad|n_{tt}|_{2}\leq M(c_{0})c_{4}^{3}.

Proof.

First, note that (
5.21
) implies that

\zeta_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{2}A_{k}(v)\partial_{k}\zeta+B^{*}(v)\zeta+\frac{1}{2}(\delta-1)h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\text{div}v=0,

where
B^{*}(v)=(\nabla v)^{\top}+\frac{(\delta-1)}{2}\text{div}v\mathbb{I}_{2}
. Thus one can easily obtain

|\zeta(t)|_{4}\leq Cc_{0}\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{3}.

Second,
n=(ah)^{b}
implies that

n_{t}+v\cdot\nabla n+(2-\delta-\gamma)a^{b}h^{b}\text{div}v=0,

this, together with Lemmas
5.3
-
5.5
and (
5.2
), implies that for
0\leq t\leq T_{3}
,

\displaystyle|n|_{\infty}\leq a^{b}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{-b}\leq M(c_{0}),\quad|\nabla n|_{q}=a^{b}|bh^{b-1}\nabla h|_{q}\leq M(c_{0}),\quad

\displaystyle|\nabla n|_{\infty}=a^{b}|bh^{b-1}\nabla h|_{\infty}\leq M(c_{0}),\quad|\nabla n|_{4}=2a^{b}|bh^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{4}\leq M(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}n|_{2}\leq C(|h^{b-1}\nabla^{2}h|_{2}+|h^{b-1}\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{2})\leq M(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|\nabla^{3}n|_{2}\leq C(|h^{b-1}\nabla^{3}h|_{2}+|h^{b-2}\nabla^{2}h\cdot\nabla h|_{2}+|h^{b-2}\nabla h\cdot\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{2})\leq M(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|n_{t}|_{\infty}\leq C(|v|_{\infty}|\nabla n|_{\infty}+|h^{b}|_{\infty}|\text{div}v|_{\infty})\leq M(c_{0})c_{4},

\displaystyle|n_{t}|_{2}\leq C(|v|_{4}|\nabla n|_{4}+|h^{b}|_{\infty}|\text{div}v|_{2})\leq M(c_{0})c_{2},

\displaystyle|\nabla n_{t}|_{2}\leq C(|\nabla(v\cdot\nabla n)|_{2}+|\nabla(h^{b}\text{div}v)|_{2})\leq M(c_{0})c_{4},

\displaystyle|\nabla n_{t}|_{4}\leq C(|\nabla(v\cdot\nabla n)|_{4}+|\nabla(h^{b}\text{div}v)|_{4})\leq M(c_{0})c_{4},

\displaystyle|n_{tt}|_{2}\leq C(|(v\cdot\nabla n)_{t}|_{2}+|(h^{b}\text{div}v)_{t}|_{2})\leq M(c_{0})c_{4}^{3}.

The proof of Lemma
5.6
is complete.

∎

5.2.4.
Estimates on
l
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For
T_{4}=\min\{T_{3},(1+Cc_{4})^{-10-4\nu}\}
and
t\in[0,T_{4}]
, one has

\displaystyle|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{s}|_{2}^{2}{\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0}),\quad|(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\nu},

(5.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|l_{s}|_{2}^{2}{\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\nu},\quad\int_{0}^{t}(|h\nabla^{2}l|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla^{2}l|^{2}_{2}){\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{3\nu}.

Proof.

First,
(3.1)_{3}
implies

\displaystyle w^{-\nu}(l_{t}+v\cdot\nabla l)-a_{4}h\Delta l=a_{5}ng^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w\text{div}\psi+w^{-\nu}\Pi(l,h,w,g),

(5.27)

and

\displaystyle w^{-\nu}((l-\bar{l})_{t}+v\cdot\nabla(l-\bar{l}))-a_{4}h\Delta(l-\bar{l})=a_{5}ng^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w\text{div}\psi

(5.28)

\displaystyle+w^{-\nu}\Pi(l-\bar{l},h,w,g),

where

H(v)=2\alpha\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\partial_{i}v^{(i)})^{2}+\beta(\text{div}v)^{2}+\alpha\sum_{i\neq j}^{2}(\partial_{i}v^{(j)})^{2}+2\alpha v_{2}^{(1)}v_{1}^{(2)}.

Multiplying (
5.28
) by
l-\bar{l}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, it follows from (
5.2
) and Lemmas
5.3
-
5.6
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}+a_{4}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a_{4}\int\nabla h\cdot\nabla(l-\bar{l})(l-\bar{l})-\int w^{-\nu}v\cdot\nabla(l-\bar{l})(l-\bar{l})+a_{5}\int ng^{2}H(v)(l-\bar{l})

\displaystyle+a_{6}\int w\text{div}\psi(l-\bar{l})+\int w^{-\nu}\Pi(l-\bar{l},h,w,g)(l-\bar{l})+\frac{1}{2}\int(w^{-\nu})_{t}|(l-\bar{l})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|\psi|_{\infty}+|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|v|_{\infty})|\varphi|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l-\bar{l})|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big(|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{4}^{2}+|w^{1+\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}(|\nabla\psi|_{2}+|\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}_{4})

\displaystyle+|w^{-1+\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4}^{2}\big)|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}

\displaystyle+C|w^{-1}|_{\infty}|w_{t}|_{\infty}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})c_{3}^{\nu+10}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}+M(c_{0})+\frac{a_{4}}{2}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2},

where we have used the following estimates

\displaystyle|g\nabla v|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C|g\nabla v|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla(g\nabla v)|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(|g\nabla^{2}v|_{2}+|\nabla g|_{\infty}|\nabla v|_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cc_{3}^{\frac{3}{2}},

(5.29)

\displaystyle|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla(g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w)|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w|_{2}+|\nabla g|_{\infty}|g^{-1}|_{\infty}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cc_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle|w_{t}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|w_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{2}|\nabla w_{t}|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}|\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq|w_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{2}|g^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla w_{t}|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}|g^{-1}|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\infty}\leq Cc_{2}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}w_{t}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Thus, (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and Gronwall’s inequality give

|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(l-\bar{l})|_{2}^{2}\text{d}s\leq M(c_{0})\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{4}.

Moreover,
(
5.2
) and Lemma
5.3
imply

|l-\bar{l}|_{2}^{2}\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\nu},\quad\int_{0}^{t}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}^{2}\text{d}s\leq M(c_{0}).

Multiplying (
5.27
) by
l_{t}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, it follows from (
5.2
), (
5.29
), Lemmas
5.3
-
5.6
that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{4}}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}^{2}+|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a_{4}\int\nabla h\cdot\nabla ll_{t}-\int w^{-\nu}v\cdot\nabla ll_{t}+a_{5}\int ng^{2}H(v)l_{t}

\displaystyle+a_{6}\int w\text{div}\psi l_{t}+\int w^{-\nu}\Pi(l,h,w,g)l_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\int a_{4}h_{t}|\nabla l|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|\psi|_{\infty}+|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|v|_{\infty})|\varphi|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big(|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{4}^{2}+|w^{1+\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}(|\nabla\psi|_{2}+|\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}_{4})

\displaystyle+|w^{-1+\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4}^{2}\big)|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}+C|\varphi|_{\infty}|h_{t}|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})c_{4}^{\nu+10}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}^{2}+M(c_{0})+\frac{1}{2}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}^{2},

which along with (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and Gronwall’s inequality imply

|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{s}|_{2}^{2}\text{d}s\leq M(c_{0})\quad\text{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq T_{4}.

Moreover, (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.3
gives

|\nabla l|_{2}^{2}\leq M(c_{0}),\quad\int_{0}^{t}|l_{s}|_{2}^{2}\text{d}s\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\nu}.

(5.30)

On the other hand, (
5.27
) implies that

\displaystyle-a_{4}\Delta\big(h(l-\bar{l})\big)

\displaystyle=-a_{4}h\Delta l-a_{4}F(\nabla h,l-\bar{l})=w^{-\nu}\mathcal{A}-a_{4}F(\nabla h,l-\bar{l}),

(5.31)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=-\left(l_{t}+v\cdot\nabla l\right)+a_{5}w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)+a_{6}w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi+\Pi(l-\bar{l},h,w,g),

(5.32)

\displaystyle F=F\big(\nabla h,l-\bar{l}\big)=(l-\bar{l})\Delta h+2\nabla h\cdot\nabla l.

Then (
5.1
), (
5.2
), (
5.30
) and Lemma 3.2-
5.6
imply

\displaystyle|\mathcal{A}|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(|l_{t}|_{2}+|v|_{\infty}|\nabla l|_{2}+|w^{\nu}|_{\infty}|n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{4}^{2}+|w^{\nu+1}|_{\infty}|\nabla\psi|_{2}

(5.33)

\displaystyle+|w^{\nu+1}|_{\infty}|\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}|_{4}^{2}+|w^{\nu}|_{\infty}|\psi|_{\infty}|\nabla l|_{2}+|w^{\nu-1}|_{\infty}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})(|l_{t}|_{2}+c_{1}^{1+\nu}),

\displaystyle|F|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(|\nabla^{2}h|_{4}|l-\bar{l}|_{4}+|\psi|_{\infty}|\nabla l|_{2})\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\frac{\nu}{4}},

where we have used the fact that for
0\leq t\leq T_{4}

\displaystyle\|v\|_{2}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|v_{s}\|_{2}^{2}\text{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq M(c_{0})(1+c_{4}t^{\frac{1}{2}})\leq M(c_{0}),

(5.34)

\displaystyle|g\nabla v|_{4}\leq

\displaystyle|g(0,x)\nabla u_{0}|_{4}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}|(g\nabla v)_{s}|_{4}^{2}\text{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|h_{0}\nabla u_{0}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla(h_{0}u_{0}\big)|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(|g_{s}|_{\infty}^{2}|\nabla v|_{4}^{2}+|g\nabla v_{s}|_{4}^{2})\text{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|g\nabla v_{s}|_{2}|\nabla(g\nabla v_{s})|_{2}\text{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq M(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4}\leq

\displaystyle|g^{\frac{1}{2}}(0,x)\nabla l_{0}|_{4}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|(g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w)_{s}|_{4}^{2}\text{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{0}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla(h_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{0})|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big(|g^{-\frac{1}{2}}g_{s}|^{2}_{\infty}|\nabla w|_{4}^{2}+|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{s}|_{4}^{2}\big)\text{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{s}|_{2}|\nabla(g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{s})|_{2}\big)\text{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq M(c_{0}),

\displaystyle|l-\bar{l}|_{4}\leq

\displaystyle|l-\bar{l}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla l|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\frac{\nu}{4}}.

Then (
5.33
), Lemma
2.4
and Lemma
5.5
imply

\displaystyle|h(l-\bar{l})|_{D^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(|w^{-\nu}\mathcal{A}|_{2}+|F(\nabla h,l-\bar{l})|_{2})

(5.35)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})(c_{1}^{2\nu+1}+c_{1}^{\nu}|l_{t}|_{2}),

\displaystyle|h\nabla^{2}l|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(|h(l-\bar{l})|_{D^{2}}+|\nabla^{2}h(l-\bar{l})|_{2}+|\nabla l\cdot\nabla h|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(|h(l-\bar{l})|_{D^{2}}+|\nabla^{2}h|_{4}|l-\bar{l}|_{4}+|\psi|_{\infty}|\nabla l|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})(c_{1}^{2\nu+1}+c_{1}^{\nu}|l_{t}|_{2}).

Consequently, (
5.35
) together with (
5.30
), yield that
\eqref{802}_{2}
holds for
0\leq t\leq T_{4}
.

∎

Lemma 5.8
.

For
T_{5}=\min\{T_{4},(1+Cc_{4})^{-16-8\nu}\}
and
t\in[0,T_{5}]
, one has

\displaystyle|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{s}|_{2}^{2}+|\nabla l_{s}|^{2}_{2}){\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq M(c_{0}),

(5.36)

\displaystyle|l_{t}|_{2}+|h\nabla^{2}l|_{2}+|l|_{D^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{2\nu+1},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(|h\nabla^{3}l|_{2}^{2}+|h\nabla^{2}l|_{D^{1}}^{2}+|l|^{2}_{D^{3}}){\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{2\nu+2}.

Proof.

Applying
\partial_{t}
to
(3.1)_{3}
, one has

\displaystyle l_{tt}-a_{4}w^{\nu}h\Delta l_{t}=

\displaystyle-(v\cdot\nabla l)_{t}+a_{4}(w^{\nu})_{t}h\Delta l+a_{4}w^{\nu}h_{t}\Delta l

(5.37)

\displaystyle+a_{5}(w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v))_{t}+a_{6}(w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi)_{t}+\Pi(l,h,w,g)_{t}.

Multiplying (
5.37
) by
w^{-\nu}l_{t}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}l_{t}|_{2}^{2}+a_{4}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{t}|_{2}^{2}

(5.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int(v\cdot\nabla l)_{t}w^{-\nu}l_{t}+a_{4}\int(w_{t}^{\nu}h\Delta l+w^{\nu}h_{t}\Delta l)w^{-\nu}l_{t}

\displaystyle+\int a_{5}\big(w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)\big)_{t}w^{-\nu}l_{t}+\int a_{6}(w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi)_{t}w^{-\nu}l_{t}

\displaystyle+|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}|l_{t}|_{4}|\nabla\psi|_{4}+|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\psi|_{\infty}|\nabla l_{t}|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M(c_{0})(c_{1}^{\nu}|l_{tt}|_{2}+c_{4}^{5\nu+10}).

Moreover, (
5.48
) and (
5.51
) implies
\eqref{704}_{3}
,
and
the proof of Lemma
5.9
is complete.

∎

Lemma 5.10
.

For
T_{7}=\min\{T_{6},\big(1+M(c_{0})c_{5}\big)^{-40-10\nu}\}
and
t\in\left[0,T_{7}\right]
, one has

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}|l_{t}(t)|_{D^{2}}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}l_{t}(t)|_{2}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}|h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}(t)|_{2}\leq M(c_{0})c_{1}^{\frac{\nu}{2}},

(5.52)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}s(|l_{ss}|_{D^{1}}^{2}+|h^{\frac{1}{4}}l_{ss}|_{D^{1}}^{2}){\rm{d}}s\leq M(c_{0}),\quad\frac{1}{2}c_{0}^{-1}\leq l\leq\frac{3}{2}c_{0}.

Proof.

First, applying
\partial_{t}
to (
5.37
), one has

\displaystyle l_{ttt}-a_{4}w^{\nu}h\Delta l_{tt}+(v\cdot\nabla l)_{tt}

(5.53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2a_{4}(w^{\nu}h)_{t}\Delta l_{t}+2a_{4}(w^{\nu})_{t}h_{t}\Delta l+a_{4}(w^{\nu})_{tt}h\Delta l+a_{4}w^{\nu}h_{tt}\Delta l

\displaystyle+a_{5}\big(w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)\big)_{tt}+a_{6}(w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi)_{tt}+\Pi(l,h,w,g)_{tt}.

Multiplying (
5.53
) by
w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+a_{4}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{tt}|_{2}^{2}

(5.54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int(v\cdot\nabla l)_{tt}w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}+\int\big(2a_{4}(w^{\nu}h)_{t}\Delta l_{t}+2a_{4}(w^{\nu})_{t}h_{t}\Delta l\big)w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle+\int a_{4}(w^{\nu})_{tt}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta lw^{-\nu}l_{tt}+\Big(a_{4}w^{\nu}h_{tt}\Delta l+a_{5}\big(w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)\big)_{tt}\Big)w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle+\int\big(a_{6}(w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi)_{tt}+a_{7}(w^{\nu+1}h^{-1}\psi\cdot\psi)_{tt}+a_{8}(w^{\nu}\nabla l\cdot\psi)_{tt}

\displaystyle+a_{9}(w^{\nu-1}g\nabla w\cdot\nabla w)_{tt}\big)w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle-a_{4}\int\nabla h^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\nabla l_{tt}l_{tt}+\frac{1}{2}\int(w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}})_{t}|l_{tt}|^{2}=\sum_{i=13}^{20}I_{i},

where

\displaystyle I_{13}=

\displaystyle-\int(v\cdot\nabla l)_{tt}w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle+C|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|v|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{tt}|_{2}|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2},

\displaystyle I_{14}=

\displaystyle I_{15}=

\displaystyle\int a_{5}\big(w^{\nu}ng^{2}H(v)\big)_{tt}w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}|n_{t}|_{2}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|g_{t}|_{\infty}|g\nabla v|_{\infty}|\nabla v|_{\infty}\big)|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2}+\int ng^{2}H^{t}(v)h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle+C|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}\big(|n|_{\infty}|g\nabla v_{t}|_{4}^{2}+|g\nabla v_{t}|_{4}(|n|_{\infty}|w^{-1}|_{\infty}|w_{t}|_{4}|g\nabla v|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+|n_{t}|_{4}|g\nabla v|_{\infty}+|n|_{\infty}|g_{t}|_{\infty}|\nabla v|_{4})\big)|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2},

\displaystyle I_{16}=

\displaystyle\int a_{6}(w^{\nu+1}\text{div}\psi)_{tt}w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1}|_{\infty}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{9}{4}}|h_{t}|_{\infty}|\psi|_{\infty}|\psi_{t}|_{2}\big)|w^{-\frac{\nu}{2}}h^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{tt}|_{2},

\displaystyle I_{18}=

\displaystyle+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}}|_{\infty}(|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\psi|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla l_{tt}|_{2}+|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\nabla l|_{\infty}|\psi_{tt}|_{2})

\displaystyle+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-1}|_{\infty}|w_{t}|_{4}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\nabla l|_{\infty}|\psi_{t}|_{4}

\displaystyle+|\varphi|_{\infty}|h^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla l_{t}|_{2}(|w^{-1}|_{\infty}|w_{t}|_{4}|\psi|_{\infty}+|\psi_{t}|_{4})\big)|l_{tt}|_{4},

\displaystyle I_{19}=

\displaystyle a_{9}\int(w^{\nu-1}g\nabla w\cdot\nabla w)_{tt}w^{-\nu}h^{-\frac{1}{2}}l_{tt}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{\infty}^{2}(|hg^{-1}|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-2}|_{\infty}|g^{-\frac{1}{4}}w_{tt}|_{2}+|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-3}|_{\infty}|w_{t}|_{4}^{2})

\displaystyle+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-1}|_{\infty}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|g_{tt}|_{4}|\nabla w|_{4}|\nabla w|_{\infty}+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-2}|_{\infty}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}(|w_{t}|_{2}|g_{t}|_{\infty}|\nabla w|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle+|w_{t}|_{\infty}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w_{t}|_{4}|g^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla w|_{4})+|w^{\frac{\nu}{2}-1}|_{\infty}|\varphi|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{4}}|g_{t}|_{\infty}|\nabla w|_{\infty}|\nabla w_{t}|_{2}
